Specifically, the testimonies provided by Olga describe a sequence of events where Wright loses a hand, is searched by Smith, and then picks up a bottle and swings it. However, the evidence, such as the crime scene photos and the autopsy report, provides a static view of the aftermath without directly detailing the dynamics of the events leading up to the crime.

**Step 2:** Checking if any spatial propositions apply to this contradiction, we can see that Proposition 5 directly relates to the action described in Testimony 3, where Wright picks up a bottle and swings it. This proposition states that if the grape juice bottle was on the table and Mr. Wright's prints on it are upside-down, then the grape juice bottle was hit from behind by Mr. Wright.

**Step 3:** Proposition 5 is applicable because it directly addresses the spatial relationship between Mr. Wright, the grape juice bottle, and the direction from which the bottle was hit. The evidence (Evidence 3: Deadly Bottle) mentions that Mr. Wright's prints on the grape juice bottle are upside-down, which, according to Proposition 5, implies that the bottle was hit from behind by Mr. Wright. However, Testimony 3 by Olga suggests that Wright picked up the bottle and swung it, which could imply a forward motion rather than hitting from behind. This creates a contradiction between the evidence (indicating a hit from behind) and the testimony (suggesting a forward swing).

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning used here involves understanding the implications of the bottle's orientation and the direction of the force applied to it. If Wright's prints are upside-down on the bottle, it suggests that the bottle was inverted when he held it, which aligns with hitting something from behind (as the bottle's opening would be facing away from him). However, Olga's testimony implies a more aggressive, forward motion, which does not necessarily align with the spatial evidence provided by the bottle's prints.

**Step 5:** Given the analysis, the contradiction lies between Evidence 3 (Deadly Bottle) and Testimony 3 (Olga's description of Wright picking up a bottle and swinging it), as they present different implications about the direction and manner in which the bottle was used.
